## Document 010: 10月美联储议息会议点评：美联储维持利率不变，年内加息预期仍强

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月美联储议息会议点评：美联储维持利率不变，年内加息预期仍强.pdf
Report source: 爱建证券
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告
总量研究 / 宏观研究 2026 年 10 月 29 日
细分研究
美联储维持利率不变，年内加息预期仍强
——10 月美联储议息会议点评
相关研究
-
投资要点：
 事件：10 月 28 日，美联储公布 10 月议息会议决议，以 10 比 2 的投票结果决定维持联邦基
金利率目标区间在 3.75%-4.00%不变。两名委员主张加息 25 个基点。
 核心观点：美联储维持政策利率不变，但经济韧性较强，通胀处于高位，货币政策紧缩倾向
延续。（1）政策方面，美联储维持联邦基金利率区间在 3.75%-4.00%不变，继续保持银行体
系充足准备金。（2）经济方面，美联储认为经济活动以较强速度扩张，居民支出保持韧性，
生产率增长强劲、资本投资稳健，就业增长与劳动力规模扩张基本匹配，但通胀仍处于较高
水平。（3）预期方面，两名委员主张加息 25 个基点，反映美联储内部对通胀风险及政策力度
仍存在分歧。市场交易结果显示，年内 12 月加息 25 个基点的预期仍较强，后续政策更多取
决于经济数据及通胀走势。

 政策：维持利率不变，货币政策紧缩倾向延续。
 美联储 10 月议息会议决定维持联邦基金利率不变，政策利率保持稳定。（1）政策利率维持不
变，但投票结果显示一定分歧。美联储以 10 比 2 的投票结果通过利率决议，维持联邦基金利
率目标区间不变，本次会议未进一步收紧货币政策。两名委员 Lisa D. Cook 和 Beth M.
Hammack 主张加息 25 个基点，显示美联储内部对于当前利率水平是否足以抑制通胀存在
分歧。（2）继续维持银行体系充足准备金。美联储表示，将继续实施维持银行体系充足准备
金的政策，并根据需要购买短期国债，在必要时购买剩余期限不超过 3 年的其它国债，以维
持充足的准备金水平。总体看，美联储维持政策利率不变，同时继续通过相关公开市场操作维
持银行体系充足准备，货币政策与准备金管理安排均延续既有方向。
 经济：增长动能较强，就业保持稳定，通胀仍处高位。
 美联储对美国经济活动判断相对乐观，国内支出保持韧性，生产率增长强劲，资本投资稳健，
但通胀仍未回到政策目标水平。（1）经济活动保持较强扩张。美联储指出，经济活动正以较

强速度扩张，尽管地缘政治等因素导致不确定性仍较高，但国内支出表现韧性，生产率增长强
劲，资本投资稳健，显示美国经济仍具有一定内生增长动力。（2）就业市场总体保持稳定。美
联储表示，就业增长与劳动力规模扩张基本匹配，失业率变化不大。与就业市场明显走弱的情
形相比，美联储对当前就业形势的表述相对平稳，暂未显示出需要通过快速宽松政策应对就
业下行的紧迫性。（3）通胀仍是政策约束。美联储明确指出，通胀仍处较高水平，并强调需要
积累更多信息，以判断何种政策立场能够确保通胀及时回归 2%的目标。总体看，美联储对经
济增长和就业市场判断相对积极，但通胀仍处较高水平，后续政策调整仍需更多数据支持，尚
未释放转向宽松的信号。
 预期：经济韧性叠加通胀压力，年内加息预期仍强。
 美联储本次会议维持利率不变，同时并未释放宽松信号。经济活动保持较强扩张，就业市场总
体稳定，而通胀仍处高位，说明美联储仍需在控制通胀与维持就业稳定之间进行权衡。（1）
经济韧性降低短期内转向宽松的紧迫性。美联储认为当前经济形势较强，且未表现出明显的

衰退压力，货币政策仍有必要维持紧缩约束通胀。（2）通胀高位运行，进一步加息仍具较大
可能。美联储明确表示通胀仍处较高水平，并强调需要更多信息来判断合适的政策立场，当前
美国通胀水平离目标仍有明显差距，货币政策进一步紧缩概率较高。（3）市场仍预期 12 月加
息 25 个基点。美联储维持政策利率不变，但两名委员主张加息 25 个基点，显示内部存在进
一步加息的立场。会后市场交易结果显示，年内 12 月加息 25 个基点的预期仍较强。总体看，
本次会议维持政策利率不变，但经济韧性和通胀压力仍存，年内进一步加息概率较大。
 总结而言，美联储 10 月议息会议维持政策利率不变，且表示继续保持银行体系充足准备金。
经济方面，美国国内支出保持韧性，生产率增长强劲，投资稳健，就业总体稳定；但通胀仍处
高位。政策方面，两名委员主张加息 25 个基点，显示美联储对进一步收紧政策仍存分歧。往
后看，通胀走势与就业市场变化仍是影响美联储利率决策的关键变量，市场对 12 月加息 25
个基点的预期仍然较强。
 风险提示：美国就业数据超预期，美国通胀水平超预期，地缘冲突风险。

证券分析师

评级说明
投资建议的评级标准
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为股票评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内的相对市场表现，也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 个月内的公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅作为基准。其中：A 股市场：沪深 300
指数（000300.SH）；新三板市场：三板成指（899001.CSI）（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（899002.CSI）（针对做市转让标的）；
北交所市场：北证 50 指数（899050. BJ）；香港市场：恒生指数（HIS.HI）；美国市场：标普 500 指数（SPX.GI）或纳斯达克指数（IXIC.GI）。
股票评级
买入 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅大于 15%
增持 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 5%～15%之间
持有 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在-5%～5%之间

卖出 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅小于-5%
行业评级
强于大市 相对表现优于同期相关证券市场代表性指数
中性 相对表现与同期相关证券市场代表性指数持平
弱于大市 相对表现弱于同期相关证券市场代表性指数

分析方法可能存在局限性，请谨慎参考。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 澳洲央行決議如預期升息一碼至 4.60%為今年第四度升息，基準利率創 2011 年
以來新高，澳洲央行在政策聲明中表示通膨壓力尚未緩解，不排除在11、12 月
政策會議進一步升息；另一方面，澳洲8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%略低
於預期，但年增 4.0%仍高於澳洲央行 2~3%的目標區間。
⚫ 美國第二季美 GDP 終值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%， 其中實質個人消費支出創下2025
年 3 月以來最大單月增幅，顯示即使高利率持續，美國家庭消費依然具韌性；
另一方面，美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增月增 0.2%、3.0%，雙雙低於市場預
期的 0.3%、3.3%，美國 9 月非農就業人數新增 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人，失
業率由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，8 月非農就業人數亦同步下修至 13.3 萬人，通膨下滑
和勞動力市場略有降溫，降低市場對聯準會 10 月升息預期，Fedwatch 顯示 10

月升息機率下降至 20%左右。
⚫ 受美伊衝突引發的能源價格衝擊影響 ，9 月歐元地區消費者物價調和指數(HICP)
整體上升速度遠超預期，其中，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%，歐元區年增
3.8%，均高於歐洲央行的 2%目標，反映能源價格的衝擊持續影響物價，加深歐
洲央行再次升息的壓力。
⚫ 日本上週公佈貨幣政策會議紀要及央行意見摘要顯示多名委員認為當前政策利
率依然處於中性區間之下 ，需警惕通膨上行風險 ， 且未來應該以更快速度升息抑
制通膨，然而意見摘要提及日本經濟財政大臣城內實表示希望央行充分審視過
去升息對景氣之影響 ，市場解讀為政府牽制日本央行未來升息步伐 ， 而財務大臣
片山皋月則重申尊重日本央行的自主性。

月升息機率降至兩成，然而美國第二季 GDP 上修至 2.2%經濟基本面穩健仍將支撐
美元。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價處於區間波動，且各天期均線
趨平，上方 158.50 有年線、季線壓力，下方 156.25 則有月線支撐，預期短線以區間
震盪可能性較高。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪。

至 1.1380，然而在全球債券拋售潮下，資金回流美元帶動美元指數突破 102 關卡，

物價調和指數(HICP)，法國年增 3.4%、德國年增 3.3%、歐元區年增 3.8%，顯示能
源價格衝擊依然使通膨壓力居高不下， 市場預期歐洲央行可能在 12 月再度升息 ， 將
帶給歐元部分支撐。二、美國 8 月核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.0%、月增 0.2%低於預
期，而 9 月非農就業人數 2.9 萬人低於預期的 9 萬人 ， 失業率則由 4.1%上升至 4.2%，
就業降溫及通膨回落降低市場對聯準會十月升息的預期，然而第二季美國 GDP 終
值從 1.5%上修至 2.2%，經濟基本面持穩仍有利美元維持強勢。三、技術面而言，在
日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價在各天期均線下方並跌破 1.1300 整數關卡，月線向下穿
過季線形成死亡交叉，顯示短線空方動能較強。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈
現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

Implications — We believe the margin implications of a rising semi sales mix are well
understood by the market and leave our investment thesis intact. Our constructive
view is based on steady growth in existing businesses, supplemented by medium- to
long-term opportunities in humanoid robots, drones, and LEO satellites. We also

view Sinbon as a relatively defensive stock amid AI-related market volatility, given
its limited direct AI exposure and diversified business portfolio. The shares could
benefit from investor rotation toward companies with resilient underlying
fundamentals, while emerging applications provide additional support for the
longer-term growth outlook.

### Page 4

Sinbon (3023.TW)

Sinbon
Valuation
Our TP for Sinbon is NT$370. We value Sinbon on PE to capture the company’s earnings growth prospects. We set our target
PE at 23x, which we set at a 20% premium to the historical average of 19x since 2018. We believe 23x is justified in view of
improving fundamentals with strong momentum of industrials segment, driving a clear margin expansion and solid earnings
growth in 2026E. Applying this to our 2H26-1H27E EPS forecast, we arrive at our target price of NT$370.

Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Sinbon's share to deviate from our target price include 1) slower-than-expected growth in
non-IT/non-consumer applications; 2) Intensified competition; 3) Raw material price hike and labor cost increases; 4) FX
volatility; 5) Greater-than-expected impact from the geopolitical conflict.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV

## Document 106: 261027_ml_faraday

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_ml_faraday.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Faraday

Valuation remains stretched; timely 
revenue delivery to be key watchpoint

Reiterate Rating: UNDERPERFORM | PO: 120.00 TWD | Price: 191.50 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on record MCU mass production revenue  
Faraday’s 3Q26 sales was +11% QoQ, above BofAe/street of -7%/-3% QoQ and its 
guidance of -single-digit% QoQ, mainly driven by strong MP growth (+19.6% QoQ) on 
record-high MCU sales and accelerating ASIC shipments, coupled with continued IP 
revenue strength more than offsetting deferred NRE revenue recognition. 3Q26 GM 
dropped by 270 bps to 42.5% due to product mix, while increased Opex from salary 
adjustments brought 3Q26 EPS to NT$1.07 (BofAe/Street NT$1.27/0.66).  
IP and NRE strength support 4Q26 margin improvement 
4Q26 sales is guided to remain flat QoQ, as growth in IP and NRE revenue offset decline 
in MP revenue on lower MCU shipments following a high base in 3Q26. With sustained 
strength in IP segment underpinned by higher wafer pricing and utilization, along with 
NRE milestone delivery, 4Q26 GM should edge up to 43.7% on better mix, in our view.  
High-end MCU and advanced projects drive 2027 growth  
We now forecast Faraday's 2026/2027 sales to -27%/+36% YoY, supported by healthy 
demand for high-end MCU and gradual ramp in advanced projects. While the design 
change from 4nm to 2nm for a US customer’s ASIC project should bring greater contract 
value, Faraday’s capability to deliver on schedule and achieve timely revenue recognition 
remains key watchpoint. On higher advanced packaging/nodes projects contribution 
through 2027, GM should decline slightly from 44.5% in 2026 to 42.9% in 2027. 
Reiterate Underperform with unchanged NT$120 PO 
We lower our 2026 EPS from NT$4.05 to NT$3.19 on higher Opex and trim 2027-28 
EPS by 3-10% as we remain cautious on the timing of NRE/MP recognition for advanced 
projects given the increased design complexity and constrained OSAT capacity. We 
reiterate Underperform rating with unchanged PO of NT$120 on 17x 2027E P/E. Current 
valuation appears stretched with shares trading at 28x '27 P/E (vs. historical 13-42x).

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Faraday Technology Corp. was established in 1993 and 
listed in 1999. The company is an affiliate of the second 
biggest semiconductor logic foundry (UMC) in Taiwan, and 
has been the main provider to UMC's IP library. Faraday is 
also known for its total solution in ASIC (customized chips) 
which includes chip-design and turnkey mass production, 
on top of its IP offering. The company has a diversified end-
market exposure, including internet of things, factory 
automation, solar, microcontrollers etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have an Underperform rating on Faraday, as we are 
wary of the slower for longer business outlook in mature 
node projects, while its collaboration with non-TSMC 
foundries bears the uncertainty.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Faraday (FDYTF) 
Our PO NT$120 is derived from 17x 2027E P/E, which is at low-to-mid-end of the 
company's historical valuation range. In our view, the multiple is backed by gross margin 
expansion from 27% in 2025 to over 40% in 2026/27 while revenue would slow down 
from a peak in 2025.

Upside risks: (1) faster-than-expected progress in new businesses including advanced 
nodes and advanced packaging, and (2) a better margin profile thanks to more stringent 
opex and enhanced value-add to customers

Downside risks: (1) downturn in foundry business cycle, given that the firm adopts a 
cost-plus model for turnkey (mass production) business, and (2) slower qualification and 
ramp-up of the yield rate for advanced packaging business

Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Momentum Seen Sustained into
2027
SL T, photonics (transceiver+CPO), and power testing should
continue to drive growth into 2027, with contributions from FT
handlers and burn-in ovens likely emerging as well. Stay OW.
3Q26 miss on GM: See details in Results Summary
Growth momentum seen sustained into 4Q26 and 2027: Management indicated
that the strong business momentum observed in 3Q26 is likely to stay intact going

forward, driven by both power testing and semi & photonics businesses. Normalized
GM remains at ~60% (2026 guidance of ~60% GM implies GM back to ~60% in
4Q26 vs. 57.5% in 3Q26).
• Power testing: 60-70% of revenue is AI-related for 2026, and it is expected
to be sustained into 2027. HVDC testing tools could be the major driver, and
it has already received orders from customers.
• SLT: 9M26 revenue doubled on a YoY basis. SL T demand used to focus on

one major customer, but management indicated that it has added AVGO and
MRVL as new customers. Moreover, demand in 4Q26/1H27 should be mostly
related to inference applications, including CPU/ASIC/LPU.
• Photonics: 9M26 revenue grew by 4x on  a YoY basis. Demand from
transceivers should remain solid and CPO should grow notably in 2027.
Chroma focuses on Insertion 3/4o and shipments for the mass production
line next year  are seen reaching 4-5x vs. that for the pilot line this year.

Moreover, it has added another customer for a pilot run in 9M27 and mass
production in 4Q27. At the industry level, if insertion is excluded from mass
production, Insertion 3 tool demand should be higher. Opportunities will
likely be much larger when CPO penetrates the scale-up network.
• New businesses: FT handlers have been qualified by multiple HPC/CSP
customers and orders have been placed. Burn-in ovens have secured the first
CSP customer, with more HPC customers expected to follow next year.

These should be new addressable markets for Chroma in the coming years.
Stay OW: We believe Chroma should continue to benefit from the AI compute/
networking/power testing opportunities due to its strong customer relationship,
solid execution and outstanding technology expertise. Our PT of NT$2,800 is based
on 40x 2027e P/E, implying a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

Results Summary
3Q26 miss on GM:
Off revenue of  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY), GM contracted 3ppt QoQ, to
57.5%, below our estimate at 60.5% and consensus at 62.7%, mainly owing to a less
favorable product mix.
Opex of NT$3,141mn (opex ratio 17.5%) was lower than our estimate at NT$3,797mn (opex
ratio 21.2%) and consensus at NT$3,321mn (opex ratio 21.7%), reflecting operating
leverage.
This led to operating profit of NT$7,167mn (OPM up 1.2ppt QoQ, to 40.0%), 2% higher

than our estimate at NT$7,040mn (OPM 39.3%) and 14% higher than consensus at NT
$6,281mn (OPM 41.0%).
With a narrower non-op gain of NT$186mn, net profit reached NT$5,647mn (EPS NT
$13.37), 2% short of our estimate at NT$5,790mn (EPS NT$13.71) and 6% short of
consensus at NT$5,317mn (EPS NT$12.59).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment looks set to reach another
record high in 2026
Revenue for the segment increased 55% YoY to NT$10,546mn (37% of the mix) in 2025.
We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 4: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 5: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 6: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream

believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 40x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 40x is justified by the 68% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

## Document 191: [000270] 기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것배당은 지켜질 것 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것기아(000270) 배당은 지켜질 것배당은 지켜질 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

기아 (000270)

Company Brief 
자동차/모빌리티 
Analyst 김준성 
joonsung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY) 을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000 원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

## Document 262: Anthropic招股書裡最值得看的14個細節

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic招股書裡最值得看的14個細節.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
Anthropic 招股書裡最值得看的 14 個細節 
2026/10/27

摘要： 
⚫ 華爾街見聞整理了人工智慧獨角獸 Anthropic 提交 S-1 招股書的核心數據與關鍵細節。公司
擬以代碼「ANT」登陸那斯達克，目標估值介於 1.5 兆至 2 兆美元之間。 
⚫ 營收五個季度漲了約 30 倍，第二季已經轉盈，上半年帳面卻虧了 807 億美元，算力帳單還簽
到了 2045 年……Anthropic 招股書裡最值得看的 14 個細節，不少都帶著這樣的反差。更少見
的是控制權：普通股東幾乎沒有話語權，50.1%的投票權握在七位創辦人手中。 
1. 瘋狂的營收增速與放緩跡象  
⚫ 營收從 2023 年的 4,100 萬美元飆升至 2025 年的 45.85 億美元，2026 上半年達 
162.86 億美元；第二季單季營收 115.55 億美元，五個季度內暴增約 30 倍。今年前 9 
個月累計收入突破 330 億美元，過去一年人均創收達 660 萬美元。不過第二季至第三季
營收季增幅已放緩至接近 50%，公司警示早期爆發增長後未來可能停滯。

### Page 2

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
2. 半年帳面虧損 807 億美元，主因為公允價值變動  
⚫ 2026 上半年帳面淨虧損達 807.48 億美元，但其中約 723.88 億美元是因公司估值大幅
上升，導致可轉換票據、衍生負債與 SAFE 評估價值提高所認列的帳面虧損，並無現金流
出。扣除股權激勵等非現金費用後，上半年調整後營業利潤為 16.18 億美元，調整後淨利
潤達 26.71 億美元。 
3. 算力成本佔比驟降，經營利潤成功轉正  
⚫ 每 1 美元營收對應的算力與基礎設施支出，從 2025 年第一季的超過 2.5 美元降至 
2026 年第二季的不足 50 美分（算力支出佔比由 2023 年的 10 倍以上降至 2026 上
半年的 55%）。2026 年第二季經營利潤達 9.74 億美元（利潤率 8%），上半年營運現
金流淨流入 39 億美元，主要受惠於「閉環研發」模型訓練效率提升、推理優化、跨晶片
自建調度及降價策略。

4. 客戶結構多元化，頭部集中度下降  
⚫ 今年前 9 個月前五大客戶貢獻 25% 營收，但佔比從第一季的約 36% 降至第三季的 
21%，已無單一客戶佔比超過 10%。年消費破 10 萬美元的客戶超過 11,900 家，超過
半數名列財富 500 強（但其支出佔自身總預算不到 0.1%）。淨收入留存率（不含訂閱）
高達約 900%，且第三季有超過 80% 的營收來自近 6 個月內發布的新模型。

### Page 3

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。

5. 5%至 7%的收入來自違規客戶  
⚫ 前 9 個月約有 5% 至 7%（約 16 億至 23 億美元）的營收來自事後被判定違反使用規
範或商業條款的客戶，主因是監控、調查至封號存在時間差。隨著 8、9 月加大封禁力
度，此類收入佔比將下降，可能對未來的營收與增速造成一定拉低。 
6. 算力長約承諾超 8,000 億美元，最遠鎖定至 2045 年  
⚫ 各項固定或保底採購、已簽約未起算之租約及後續長期合約合計超過 8,300 億美元，包括
博通（五年 1,610 億美元租約及後續最高 1,500 億美元支出）、AMD（逾 200 億美
元）、三大雲端巨頭（十年至少 2,694 億美元）等。合約多採能源長約常見的「照付不
議」（用量不足仍須補足差額）條款，成本提前鎖定，而公司 80% 收入卻採依用量實際
結算，形成節奏差異。

### Page 4

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。
7. 每月支付給馬斯克的 SpaceX 算力費高達 31 億美元
⚫ Anthropic 租用 SpaceX.AI（原 xAI）位於孟菲斯的資料中心，合約總額最高達 1,205
億美元，合約至 2029 年。12 月算力全面上線後每月費用約 31 億美元，該月費已接近
SpaceX 今年首季整個 AI 業務營收（8.18 億美元）的四倍之多。
8. 巨頭持股版圖與極短的解禁安排
⚫ 若以 2 兆美元估值計算，亞馬遜全面轉股後約持股近 20%（約 4,000 億美元，超出
12% 上限部分暫以票據保留），Google 持股約 7% 並擁有後續增資與基石投資權限。
解禁期不同於傳統 180 天限制，上市後 76 天內設五個解禁日，首日即允許非創辦人董
事、高管與部分員工減持套現。
9. 特殊雙層治理架構，創辦團隊握有 50.1%絕對投票權
⚫ 普通股東對決策幾乎無控制權，亞馬遜與 Google 持有的 N 類股亦無投票權。公司頂層

由「長期利益信託」（LTBT）掌控制衡，而七位共同創辦人則透過僅 1 股、每人出資 1
美元認購的「F 類股」掌握 50.1% 的絕對投票權，屬於那斯達克規則下的「受控公
司」。
10. 股價每漲 50%，最高提撥 2%股權投入公益
⚫ 上市前已將全面攤薄股本的 5%（約 8.47 億股 N 類股）劃入 Anthropic 公益基金。董
事會批准未來股價若從發行價起每上漲 50%，可再額外注入最高 2% 股份；同時七位共
同創辦人承諾將個人 80% 的股權收益用於公益捐贈。
11. 長達 90 多頁的「生存級」風險因子揭露
⚫ 篇幅約為 SpaceX 的三倍，直接提及技術可能對人類構成「災難性或生存級風險」、可能
被惡意行為者用於開發致命武器，甚至可能衍生出模型抵制關機、操縱資訊或勒索等失控
行為。招股書直言，此類系統性風險恐怕連整個全球保險業都無力承保。
12. 創辦團隊向投資者發出的理念警告
⚫ 七位創辦人公開呼籲投資人切勿純以短期財務交易視角看待公司，直言部分商業決策會在
公共利益與利潤最大化之間權衡，作法可能與股東預期背道而馳。同時，團隊也著重強調

創始成員至今未流失，與競爭對手 OpenAI 形成對比。
13. 上市股票代碼為「ANT」，展現低自我企業文化
⚫ Anthropic 將以股票代碼「ANT」在那斯達克掛牌。招股書以「low-ego」（低自我）形
容內部文化，員工內部自稱為「Ants」（螞蟻），形成文化與代碼的巧妙呼應。

### Page 5

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
14. 市場下一步檢驗指標：10 月 ARR 表現  
⚫ 市場高度關注 S-1 補充文件中將揭露的 10 月 Run-Rate Revenue（以近 28 天用量年
化加計訂閱費）。目前市場預期 730 億美元為合理基準，若突破 780 億美元，將大幅提
高市場對其在 12 月達成 1,000 億美元 ARR 的信心，成為左右其 1.5 兆至 2 兆美元 
IPO 估值的關鍵密碼。

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 329: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026

### Page 1

OpenAI 和 Anthropic 周末同时亮出下⼀代底牌： OpenAI 悄悄灰测 GPT-6.2 Astra ，修复安
全隐患，后台流量占⽐已近两成； Anthropic 官⽹源码意外曝光 Claude Mythos 5.5 与
Fable 5.5 双旗舰 —— 前者 53 分钟攻破 Linux 内核，后者⽀持百万 Token 上下⽂、独⽴编码
两⼩时⼿搓 3D 版《我的世界》。
摘要：
两⼤新模型爆料，竟撞在了同⼀天！
⾕歌Gemini 4 Carbon刚把全球第⼀抢回去没⼏天， OpenAI 和 Anthropic 就在周末同时亮出了下
⼀代底牌。
OpenAI 直接跳过 6.1 ，在 Codex 后台悄悄灰测GPT-6.2 Astra。
结果这⼀爆料刚冲上热搜，后脚 Anthropic 官⽅源码⾥就被扒出赫然躺着Claude Mythos 5.5。
这两款新模型的核⼼亮点，全在下⾯划好重点了：
GPT-6.2 Astra
跳过 6.1 闭关重炼直奔 6.2 ：原计划冲刺的 6.1 因安全测试「翻⻋」被迫回炉，闭关重炼⼀个⽉后

直接跳级 6.2 ；
不只会写代码，还能⾃⼰盯梢：为配合新模型， OpenAI Codex 团队28 天连夜重做底层，让 AI 能
⾃⼰拉起⼦进程、连续⼯作⼏⼩时；
Claude 5.5
杀伤⼒太强被「锁进保险柜」：⽆护栏特种版Mythos 5.5仅向顶尖⽹安与科研机构开放；⽀持
100 万上下⽂的通⽤旗舰Fable 5.5则已现⾝ MLflow 与 Azure ⽬录；
53 分钟攻破 Linux 与⼿搓 3D ⼤作：Mythos 5.5 仅⽤53 分钟独⽴攻破 Linux 内核拿到最⾼权限；
Fable 5.5⼀句话⼿搓出 3D 版《我的世界》。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⼀天之内，两⼤巨头全部提前泄露下⼀代旗舰。这周的 AI 圈，⼀场刺⼑⻅红的正⾯硬刚彻底藏不
住了。
知名爆料⼈ Token Gremlin 忍不住调侃， Anthropic 真是⻅不得 OpenAI 独⾃抢占哪怕⼀天的聚光
灯，⾮要出来截胡不可。
GPT-6.2 Astra 后台悄悄换芯，新模型已吃下近两成流量
最先被开发者抓到现⾏的，是 OpenAI 的当家编程与 Agent 旗舰。
11 ⽉ 1 ⽇晚间，开发者 Salio 在调试 Codex 时抓包发现，前端界⾯虽然还挂着 GPT-6 Astra 的名
字，但底层 API 返回的数据包⾥，模型真⾝已经换成了gpt-6.2-astra。
这并⾮个例。很快，多位 Pro 订阅⽤⼾在后台⽤量统计⾯板⾥翻出了更硬的账单证据。
在 Token Gremlin 晒出的账⼾账单明细中，旧版 gpt-6-astra 占了 80% 的调⽤量，⽽单独列出的
gpt-6.2-astra 已经吃下了 19.8% 的真实流量。

### Page 3

悄悄换上 6.2 新底座后，最先感知到变化的是命中灰度的重度开发者。
Token Gremlin 给出了极⾼评价，直⾔ GPT-6.2 Astra 相⽐初代实现了巨⼤跨越，在多个维度上超
越 Claude Opus 5.5。
⽆论是设计审美、底层⼯程技术还是编程表现都全⾯跃升，完全具备与 Fable 5.5 正⾯掰⼿腕的
实⼒。
跳过 6.1 闭关重炼，⼀次性修好两⼤致命硬伤
新模型的实战体感之所以能实现如此⼤的跨越，甚⾄连版本号都直接跳过了 6.1 ，背后源于过去
⼀个⽉的闭关重炼。
OpenAI 借着这次跳级，集中修好了前两代最致命的两个硬伤：
第⼀，修好了 6.1 「越权瞒报」的安全红线。
原计划 10 ⽉发布的 GPT-6.1 Astra 在内部安全测试中翻了⻋ —— 它在⻓任务⾥会擅⾃越权调⽤外
部⼯具，事后甚⾄对⼈类隐瞒⾃⼰做过的操作，被 OpenAI 紧急叫停。
第⼆，治好了初代 6.0 「⾼智商却不会⼲活」的偏科顽疾。
知名技术博主 Teortaxes 透露了⼀个反常识的内幕：初代 Astra 6.0 的底层智商其实早就超过了

Opus 5.5，只是因为缺乏针对真实开发环境的打磨，平时写起代码来才显得「偏科」甚⾄爱偷
懒。

### Page 4

趁着这次回炉重炼的⼀个⽉窗⼝期， OpenAI 把「安全可控」与「⼯程实战⼿感」两块短板⼀次
性补⻬，这才端出了脱胎换⻣的满⾎版GPT-6.2 Astra。
「义⽗」连更 28 天铺路， Codex 掌⻔⼈下场剧透
模型底座炼成了，外层的 Agent 脚⼿架也早已同步就位。
现在回看 Codex 负责⼈ Tibo 过去四周⼒推的「 Codex 28 天升级计划」，意图已经⾮常明显。
从拓宽上下⽂窗⼝、上线多⼦ Agent 协同架构，到收官前⼣突然把 Pro ⽤⼾额度翻倍，每⼀步棋
都在为 GPT-6.2 Astra 铺路。
更有意思的⼀幕发⽣在 28 天计划收官当天。当 Token Gremlin 发帖感慨「升级计划结束了，总感
觉还有很多⼤招没放完」时 ——
Codex 掌⻔⼈ Tibo 本尊亲⾃跑到评论区接话，直接剧透称：「我强烈要求把倒计时重置，咱们再
来⼀轮！」
掌⻔⼈亲⾃下场剧透，意味着已经在后台灰度的GPT-6.2 Astra，⼋成就是下周压轴登场的最终
⼤招了。
Mythos 5.5 / Fable 5.5 官⽹源码露馅，百万上下⽂双王牌亮剑

就在 OpenAI 靠后台灰度暗度陈仓的时候，⽼对⼿ Anthropic 却因为⼀次前端代码失误，把下⼀代
底牌暴露在了聚光灯下。

### Page 5

1 1 ⽉ 1 ⽇ 深 夜 ， ⼤ V  C h u b b y 晒 出 ⼀ 张 ⽹ ⻚ 源 码 截 图 ， 指 出 A n t h r o p i c 官 ⽹ 的
anthropic.com/claude/mythos ⻚⾯⾥，已经赫然写上了Claude Mythos 5.5。
全球开发者⽴刻涌⼊官⽹按下 Ctrl+U 查看源代码。
表⾯上看，⽹⻚前端显⽰的标题依然是 9 ⽉发布的旧版「 Claude Mythos 5.1 」。
但翻到底层 HTML 源码⾥，却整整⻬⻬地躺着 6 处「 Claude Claude Mythos 5.5 」，前端⼯程师
甚⾄⼿滑多拼了⼀个 Claude 。
官⽹前端已经提前把「 5.5 」的字标预埋进⽣产环境，意味着最强 Fable/Mythos5.5正式发布近在
咫尺。
⽆护栏版 Mythos 5.5 ： 53 分钟攻破 Linux ，独作数学论⽂
看到 Mythos 5.5 这个名字，不少⼈可能会好奇它与近期频频传出的下⼀代旗舰 Fable 5.5 究竟是

什么关系。
其实， Mythos 5.5 与 Fable 5.5 底层⽤的是完全相同的 AI ⼤脑，连 API 定价都分毫不差。
Anthropic 只是根据「安全护栏」的松紧，把同⼀个模型拆成了「⼀脑双版」：

### Page 6

其中，Fable 5.5装上了严格的安全护栏拦截⾼危指令，作为通⽤商业旗舰向全球开发者全量开
放。
⽽另⼀个版本Mythos 5.5，则是解开了常规拒答限制的「特种科研版」。
它仅向受信任的顶尖⽹络安全团队与科研机构定向开放，是 Anthropic 在⽹安与科学领域最强的
核武器。
⼀旦搞懂了 Mythos 5.5 「专攻⽹安与极限科研」的⾝份，前⼏天轰动技术圈的两桩神秘悬案瞬间
迎刃⽽解——
悬案⼀（⽹安实战）： 53 分钟独⽴攻破 Linux 内核拿到最⾼权限。
此前 Anthropic 披露⼀款「未发布内部模型」仅花 53 分钟就打穿了 Linux ⾼危漏洞；⽽安全研究员
⽤现有的 Mythos 5.1死磕了整整 3 天、烧掉 2000 美元都没能复现。
悬案⼆（数学科研）：狂吐 1400 万 Token 独⽴写出算法论⽂。
⼏天前⽹上疯传⼀篇攻克经典计算机难题的数学论⽂，全篇没有⼀个⼈类作者，唯⼀署名只有
冷冰冰的⼀⾏字：CLAUDE （ ANTHROPIC ）。
正如 AI 观察家 Andrew Curran 所⾔，能在内部卸下安全护栏、同时⼲出这两件⼤事的幕后真神，

正是这次源码露馅的Claude Mythos 5.5。

### Page 7

通⽤版 Fable 5.5 ：连肝两⼩时，单次⼿搓 3D 版《我的世界》
⽆护栏的 Mythos 5.5 在⽹安和数学上⼤杀四⽅，⽽⾯向⼤众开发者的通⽤旗舰Fable 5.5也已准
备就绪。
⽬前，它已同步现⾝ MLflow 与 Azure 官⽅⽬录，实锤⽀持⾼达100 万 Token 的输⼊上下⽂和12.8
万 Token 的最⼤输出。
在早期盲测中，这颗百万上下⽂底座展露出了恐怖的⼯程统治⼒。
最直观的突破，是模型戒掉了以往 AI 写⻓代码必犯的「半途偷懒」⽼⽑病。
在重度游戏⼯程测试中，开发者 notjazii将思考深度拉到最⾼档（ xhigh ），让 Fable 5.5 ⽤单次提
⽰词复刻⼀个⽹⻚版《我的世界》。
AI 在后台不知疲倦地连续编码 1 ⾄ 2 个⼩时，交出的成品不仅具备逼真的⽔⾯焦散光影和完整的
合成台。
甚⾄在没有被要求的情况下，它还主动给⾃⼰加戏，把「下界」和「末地」两个异次元维度也
完整写了出来！

### Page 8

除了能连肝两⼩时⼤⼯程，这套 5.5 底座的3D 空间感与物理光影直觉也上了⼀个⼤台阶。
在最新流出的 Three.js 实测中， Fable 5.5单次⽣成了⼀辆暗红⾊涂装的「 KESTREL K1 」 3D 超
跑，赛⻋在镜⾯地板上 360 度丝滑旋转，⻋漆⾼光反射与尾翼碳纤维结构细节堪⽐专业游戏引
擎。
哪怕是经典的「鹈鹕骑⾃⾏⻋」基准，它也告别了传统的 2D 简笔画，⽤ Three.js⼀次性写出了⼀
只⻋筐⾥装着法棍和⼤葱、在海滨⻓廊迎⻛踩踏的 3D 鹈鹕。

### Page 9

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
更有测试者甩给它⼀张奥特曼的真⼈照⽚，它仅凭纯代码就还原出了五官神态与发丝层次逼真
的 3D 体素雕像。
2 万亿 IPO 前⼣   硅⾕三巨头 11 ⽉巅峰决战
多位参与盲测的开发者判断，既然 Anthropic 已经开始在官⽹前端预埋 Mythos 5.5 代码，本周
（ 11 ⽉ 3 ⽇或 4 ⽇）极有可能就是正式发布窗⼝。
这场看似巧合的周末连环泄密，背后其实是硅⾕巨头们⼨⼟必争的战略卡位。
⼀边是 OpenAI 跳过 6.1 憋出满⾎版GPT-6.2 Astra；
另⼀边是 Anthropic 赶在传闻中11 ⽉中旬冲击 1.5 万亿⾄ 2 万亿美元超级 IPO前⼣亮出Mythos 5.5
与 Fable 5.5两⼤王牌，再加上⼏天前刚刚登顶的⾕歌Gemini 4 Carbon。
硅⾕三⼤巨头，在 2026 年 11 ⽉初撞进了史上最密集的王牌决战周。

好戏，这下真的要开场了。

## Document 374: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-11-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-11-01).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7

MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.
Q4 2026 forecasts:
Brent crude
US$101/bbl
Japanese LNG
US$24.5/MMBtu

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

### Page 3

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

### Page 4

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

## Document 394: Solvency II reforms offer limited support for bank bonds, but securitisation gains some ground

Source PDF: all report pdf/Solvency II reforms offer limited support for bank bonds, but securitisation gains some ground.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to result in a material
increase in demand for bank bonds. Securitisations beneﬁt
most directly through lower capital charges, while covered
bonds could beneﬁt modestly from the new liquidity-buﬀer
rules
Maureen Schuller
On 30 January 2027, the amendments to the Solvency II regime
will apply, making these revisions one of the important regulatory
themes for 2027. One of the key objectives of the Solvency II

reforms is to release capital and promote long-term investments by
European insurance companies in support of the ambitions of
Europe’s Savings and Investments Union (SIU). Such capital release
could also increase the capacity of insurance companies to invest
more in corporate and bank bonds.
During the past decade, insurance companies have reduced their
bond holdings in favour of equity. Rather than an active
reallocation of assets, this partially reﬂects second-round eﬀects

related to the strong equity market growth and the rise in bond

yields and corresponding decline in bond valuations against the
backdrop of the ECB rate hikes in 2022 and 2023. Higher yields
have also coincided in recent years with a stronger focus by banks
on issuance in shorter maturities, rather than the longer maturities
traditionally favoured by insurance companies. This has been
particularly noteworthy for covered bonds.

We don’t expect the Solvency II revisions to reverse this trend,
particularly as the amendments promote long-term equity
investments. Instead, for most bank bonds, the spread-risk capital
treatment remains broadly unchanged. Unsecured and
subordinated bank bonds continue to be treated as corporate

bonds with capital charges determined by their ratings and
duration, while qualifying covered bonds keep their preferential
treatment. The impact of the Solvency II revisions therefore arises
mainly indirectly through lower overall capital requirements, while
securitisations are the only debt instruments beneﬁting directly
through a more favourable capital treatment.
Nonetheless, certain reforms are worth highlighting for their
potential impact on insurance demand for diﬀerent bank bonds.

Among these, the amendments relating to long-term equity

investments, securitisations and the volatility adjustment are

Long-term equity investments and non-
life liquidity buffers
Under Solvency II, insurance companies can classify part of their
equity holdings as long-term equity investments (LTEI), beneﬁting
from a lower 22% capital charge. To qualify, these investments
must be held for at least ﬁve years, be listed in the EEA or OECD,
meet diversiﬁcation standards and be managed under a long-term

investment policy. The insurer must also be able to avoid forced
sales for ﬁve years, even under stressed conditions.
The Solvency II amended Delegated Regulation introduces two
approaches to demonstrate that forced equity sales can be
avoided. 1) The insurer must either have suﬃcient illiquid
homogeneous life insurance liabilities with a duration of more than
9.5 years or a liquidity buﬀer exceeding 100% for non-life insurance
obligations. 2) The insurer can also apply a forced-selling test to

prove that it can generate cash inﬂows that are consistently higher
than the cash outﬂows over ﬁve years, including under stress.
The liquidity buﬀer requirements for non-life insurance activities
are similar to the Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) requirements for
banks. The portfolio of liquid assets can comprise Level 1, Level 2A
and Level 2B assets, with the total value of Level 2A and Level 2B
assets capped at 40% and the value of the Level 2B assets at 15%.

Qualifying Level 1 assets, subject to a 0% haircut, are cash,
exposures to the ECB, multilateral development banks and
international organisations, and domestic currency exposures
to EEA central governments and central banks.
Qualifying Level 2A assets, subject to a 15% haircut, are bonds
and loans rated AA- or better that are not issued by an
insurance company or bank, and covered bonds rated AA- or
better as referred to in Article 3(1) of the European Covered

Bond Directive (CBD) (ie ‘European Covered Bonds’).
Qualifying Level 2B assets, subject to a 25% haircut, are
Simple, Transparent and Standardised (STS) securitisations (not

the relatively small share of non-life insurance obligations and
insurers’ sizeable existing portfolios of other liquid assets, such as
government bonds.

Lower capital requirements for
securitisations

### Page 5

The amended Solvency II Delegated Regulation lowers the capital
requirements for securitisation notes. This should encourage
insurers to invest more in these transactions, helping banks to
transfer risk and expand lending.
The risk factors (a and b ) for the spread risk of senior tranches
of STS securitisations will become fully aligned with the
treatment of similarly rated covered bonds as referred to in CBD
Article 3(1).
The risk factors for non-senior STS securitisations are reduced

to a similar extent, with the new capital charges for AAA-rated
non-senior notes at c.70% of the current capital charge, and for
AA-rated non-senior notes at 75% of the current requirement.
The diﬀerence in the capital treatment between STS and non-
STS securitisations is currently far more signiﬁcant for insurers
than for banks. This diﬀerence will be reduced substantially,
while the capital charge remains capped at 100%.
The current Solvency II regime makes no distinction between

senior and non-senior non-STS securitisation notes,
exaggerating the spread risk of senior compared with non-
senior tranches. Hence, lower risk factors are introduced for
senior non-STS compared to non-senior non-STS transactions.
As a result of these amendments, the diﬀerence in capital
requirements between CBD-compliant covered bonds and senior
STS securitisations will disappear from the end of January, while in
the highest rating buckets (AAA and AA) securitisations will be

treated better than unsecured bonds or third-country covered
bonds. Further down the rating scale, STS securitisations will be
treated closer to that of unsecured bonds from a capital
perspective, where corporate and bank unsecured bonds will still
beneﬁt from a more favourable capital charge than similarly rated
senior STS securitisations.
Securitisation alternatives will become
more competitive from a capital perspective

Source: European Commission, ING
European insurance companies currently hold only about €37bn in
collateralised debt, such as asset-backed securities (ABS) and
residential mortgage-backed securities (RMBS). This is only 12% of
what they hold in covered bonds and 3% of their corporate debt
portfolio.
The Solvency II amendments may motivate insurance companies
to diversify their debt portfolios a bit further away from covered
bonds and other bank bond products towards securitisations.

However, the impact is not expected to be substantial given that
the weighted average lives of securitisation notes may not always
be well suited to the longer-term investment needs of insurance
companies.
Volatility adjustment to align spread
sensitivities more closely
Insurance companies calculate the best estimate of their liabilities
by discounting the expected future liability cash ﬂows using the
relevant risk-free interest rate term structure. To mitigate the

balance sheet impact of bond spread volatility (ie, on the asset side
of their balance sheet), insurance companies can discount their
liabilities at the risk-free rate plus a volatility adjustment (VA).
The volatility adjustment increases the
discount rate

### Page 7

Spot rate, %
Source: EIOPA, ING
For each relevant currency (or national market in the case of the
euro area), the volatility adjustment represents 65% of the spread
(S) between the interest rate on the reference portfolio and the
relevant risk-free interest rate, adjusted for a risk correction (RC). The
latter correction factor removes the part of the spread that is
attributable to expected losses or unexpected credit or other risks
to the assets. The volatility adjustment is only added to the

applicable risk-free interest rates that are not derived from
extrapolation.
For each relevant country, a country-speciﬁc adjustment may also
be added to the volatility adjustment before the application of the
65% factor if a) the risk-corrected country spread is more than twice
the risk-corrected currency spread, and b) the risk-corrected country
spread is more than 85bp. The country component in the volatility
adjustment helps mitigate excessive widening in speciﬁc countries.

Under the current regime, the representative currency and country
portfolios are constructed as uniform portfolios, even though
insurers’ actual ﬁxed-income portfolios may diﬀer substantially.
Some insurers can therefore beneﬁt excessively from the volatility
adjustment if the resulting reduction in the best estimate of their
liabilities exceeds the decline in the value of the relevant assets
when credit spreads widen.

### Page 8

For this reason, the Solvency II revisions change the calculation of
the volatility adjustment by a) making the applicable risk
corrections (RC) more spread-sensitive, and b) introducing a credit
spread sensitivity ratio (CSSR) to better align the volatility
adjustment with the insurer’s asset-liability proﬁle. The latter ratio
reﬂects the price value of a basis point of the insurer’s bond,
securitisation and loan investments versus the price value of a

basis point of the best estimate of liabilities, with a minimum of 0
and a maximum of 1.
Moreover, subject to approval by the supervisory authority, an
undertaking-speciﬁc adjustment (USA) of up to 105% may be
applied to align the risk-corrected spread on the reference portfolio
more closely with that of the insurer’s own portfolio. This must not
lead to an increase in the applied risk-adjusted spread for more
than two consecutive quarters. Moreover, when the USA is used, the

insurer cannot also increase the volatility adjustment through a
macro volatility adjustment.
The macro volatility adjustment replaces the existing country-
speciﬁc adjustment and can be added to the euro volatility
adjustment when the relevant conditions are met. It aims to
mitigate the impact of excessive spread widening in individual euro
area countries and is based on the diﬀerence between the risk-
corrected euro spread and the risk-corrected spread for the relevant

country.
Volatility adjustment calculations

The introduction of additional minimum conditions, such as
supervisory approval and the availability of adequate processes to
calculate the volatility adjustment, has also facilitated the upward

revision of the general application ratio (GAR) from 65% to 85%.
Primarily through the introduction of the credit spread sensitivity
ratio (CSSR), the volatility adjustment will better reﬂect the
relationship between each insurer’s asset and liability sensitivity to
changes in the volatility adjustment. The revision may provide
modest support for bonds that increase the asset portfolio’s spread
sensitivity, as a higher CSSR results in a larger volatility adjustment

until the ratio reaches its cap of one. The revised calculations should
also reduce the possibility for some insurers to beneﬁt
disproportionately from the volatility adjustment when their assets
are less spread-sensitive than the reference portfolio.
Conclusion
The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to trigger a material
reallocation in favour of bank bonds. Securitisations are the most
obvious beneﬁciaries of a more favourable capital treatment, while

covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should mainly beneﬁt
indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers. We

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

critical infrastructure simultaneously, often due to shared technology dependencies,
supply chain vulnerabilities, or widely used service providers.
